# Batch positional references (S10, Owner decision 4b)

Every line the split.ts detector flagged in the split notes, with the executor's resolution. `rewrite`: the positional word(s) became "in [[note]]" (decision 4b) because the referent moved to another note of the family; `same note`: the referent stays in the note the line lands in; `external`: the reference names something outside the family; `not positional`: the trigger word does not point at a place in the note (a date, an event order, a UI position, a spreadsheet layout, a heading such as Next Steps). Line numbers are the parent's file lines before the split.

| B1 | topics/2026-06-feature-congress-r-scrapers-press-collector-pipeline | 372 | - **Optimization round (2026-06-18):** parse re-measured at ~30ms/page on typical pages (62ms was the one giant member's heavier pages) -> real run ~30min @6 cores. Stripping `<script>/<style>/<svg>` before `read_html` measured ZERO speedup (cost is core libxml2 parse, not inline JS) - ruled out, do not retry it. Six wins applied and integration-tested: (1) **shards balanced by URL count** (greedy: heaviest member -> lightest shard, via orchestrator-written `collector_plan.rds`) instead of name-hash, so no shard becomes the tail; (2) **one persistent curl pool per worker** (was per-chunk) -> HTTP/2 keepalive survives across chunks (later changed, see 2026-06-19 crash fix); (3) **retry only transient** (libcurl `fail` + HTTP 5xx); 4xx and empty-200 (JS-only) are permanent `miss`, no wasted second pass - big win on Wayback/dead-URL members; (4) orchestrator builds ONE plan (single index+cache scan) and workers read their slice -> no 6x redundant full scans, lighter workers; (5) heartbeat reads cheap cumulative "ok N" from shard logs (not re-reading all fulltext rds every 30s) + **stall guard** (break if no progress 10min -> survives a crashed worker); (6) `TOTAL_CON` 24 -> 40 (Little's law: ~0.8s latency x ~40/s core needs ~32 in-flight to not starve parse). Plan auto-unlinked at end. |  | same note | "(later changed, see 2026-06-19 crash fix)" is ### Fast pipeline = collector + scraper; collector crash fixed (2026-06-19), later in the same part -press-collector-pipeline |

Allocation now distributes the FULL $2M (score-proportional among top 133, no cap). **Committee data collected fresh from Wikipedia 119th-Congress rosters** (House Financial Services, Senate Banking, House+Senate Small Business; chairs/vice/ranking/subcommittee chairs) to a new **`Committee Data`** sheet; rubric = membership (Banking/FS=3, SmallBiz=1.5) + leadership (Chair+6, RM+4, ViceChair+3, SubChair+2.5, Financial-Institutions sub+4, SubRM+1.5), composite = 0.4·membership+0.6·leadership (later replaced by the reverse-engineered (Membership+Leadership)/2 scoring above). **REFINED (same session, later): press-gating was REMOVED** (see superseded). NOTE user's correction: **Warren & Waters ARE CU-supportive** (anti-big-bank aligns with CUs); do NOT push them down; their high committee-leader rank is correct. **FINAL board then: Tim Scott #1, French Hill #2, Roger Williams #3, Andy Barr #6, Britt #16, Steil #20, Lummis #27; enemies Durbin #490, Marshall #524, Welch #505, Gooden #523, Lofgren #326. Zero errors, $2M/133.** Remaining SOURCE-DATA (not model) gaps: see Known Issues. COM recalc tip (June guidance): force-killing a headless Excel orphan can trigger autorecover that silently rolls back the openpyxl write; verify the header/values persisted on disk after recalc; prefer try/finally cleanup over `Stop-Process`. **OneDrive race confirmed:** the file lives in OneDrive\Desktop, so an openpyxl-save immediately followed by a COM Open can race OneDrive sync and the COM crash reverts the file to a pre-write version. | B7 | actors/draft-systems-r-pipeline-architecture | 230 | - **`cu_text_collector.R`:** parse-bound problem (~62ms/page = ~16 pages/s/core; later ~30ms typical) solved by sharding members across N worker processes, each with an async libcurl multi pool (HTTP/2, multiplex), reusing `extract_release` (sourced with `CU_PR_NO_AUTORUN=1`) and writing the same `pr_checkpoints/fulltext/<bg>.rds` (url,title_detail,date_detail,body). Shards balanced by URL count via `collector_plan.rds` (greedy, deterministic, auto-unlinked at end). Fresh `new_pool()` per chunk (`CHUNK=1500`; a persistent pool crashes past ~3-4k transfers on Windows) with `rm(pool);gc(FALSE)`. Retries only transient (libcurl `fail` + 5xx); 4xx and empty-200 are permanent `miss`. Gentle recovery (<=3 passes, `total_con=8 host_con=2 robust=TRUE`, `forbid_reuse`, 5s cool-down) then chromote 403 lane (own Chrome per worker, `tc_js_fetch` polls body.innerText until >400 chars and stable, ~3.2s max; bounded by chromote `default_timeout`, not `setTimeLimit`; 0.5 s/url). `TC_CHROMOTE` gate with log line `[shard N] chromote gate: enabled=… \| M blocked(403) urls queued`; `Sys.unsetenv("CU_PR_NO_CHROMOTE")` unless `CU_TC_NO_CHROMOTE` set. Orchestrator reads `.hb`/`.done` sentinels (written via `writeLines`, `write_hb()` every 200 bodies / 10 chromote urls), 20-min idle guard on body progress, per-shard watchdog (`collector_shard_i.pid`, `kill_shard`/`spawn_shard`, `taskkill /F /T /PID`), writes `collector_residual.csv`, `CU_TC_CAP` stratified collection (always fetch `TOPIC_CORE_RX` titles; `read_index` carries title). Measured: 6 cores ~47min, 8 ~36min; real run ~30min @6 cores; RAM ~450MB/worker (6 procs ~3GB). Coverage 254,477/293,935 = 86.6% before fixes, ~97-98% projected after (~5.1k genuine 404). Self-locates via `--file=`. |  | not positional | "later ~30ms typical" is time |
